982: ## Self-review
983: 
984: **Spec coverage.** D1 (no gate change) is carried by the Global Constraints and by Task 2's two safety tests. D2 (advisory explanation) is Tasks 1 and 2. D3 (quiet period in the skill) is Task 3. D4 (build ordered last) is Task 4. The spec's success criteria map to Task 2 Step 6, Task 2 Step 1's two safety tests, Task 3 Step 4, and Task 5.
985: 
986: **Placeholders.** One value is deliberately left to be computed rather than guessed: item 94's `Verified:` digest, in Task 4 Step 6, because it is a hash of the item's own final text and cannot exist before that text does. The command that produces it is given.
987: 
988: **Type consistency.** `Get-StatusSha256` gains `Manifest` in Task 1 and Task 2 reads `$liveStatus.Manifest`. `Get-SourceManifestSidecarPath` is defined in Task 1 and called in Task 2's `Write-SourceDriftExplanation`. The sidecar path `"<mirrorPath>.source-manifest"` is written in Task 1 Step 4 and asserted as `tmp_path / "mirror.source-manifest"` in both tasks' tests.
989: 
990: ## After the plan
991: 
992: This plan does NOT bump `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`. Per `CLAUDE.md`, the bump comes after the diff debate, because the debate is what moves the tree last. The sequence after Task 5 is: whole-branch review, mode-diff debate, version bump, merge, marketplace refresh, plugin update, and an install verified by content rather than by the cache directory's name.
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window-design.md
1: # Design: the review mirror's identity window
2: 
3: **Filed 2026-09-05.** Reported from a session running the plugin against
4: KitnEssentials, which hit repeated `BLOCKED: the source status changed
5: since construction` refusals and could not tell why.
6: 
7: ## The measured facts
8: 
9: 1. `Get-StatusSha256` (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:662`) fingerprints a
10:    repo as `git status --porcelain --ignored -uall -z` PLUS the content
11:    manifest of every path that listing names.
12:    `Get-ContentManifest` (`:576`) expands a directory subject
13:    recursively, so a single `!! .claude/` status entry pulls the bytes of
14:    every file beneath it into the digest.
15: 2. The content half is deliberate and the function says so: editing an
16:    already-ignored file leaves the status listing byte-identical, so a
17:    list-only digest would pass through exactly the tampering the check
18:    exists to catch.
19: 3. The build-time `source_status_sha256` is re-checked at THREE points
20:    against the same recorded value:
21:    - `-Prepare` step 1a, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:457`
22:    - the wrapper, before the client runs, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:275`
23:    - the wrapper, after the client finishes,
24:      `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:310`, `throw 'the mirror changed while the
25:      round ran'`
26: 4. Therefore the exposed window is **mirror build to wrapper exit**, not
27:    build to prepare. It spans the whole round.
28: 5. The refusal names no path. `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:846` prints the
29:    class of change and stops.
30: 6. Nothing in `skills/` states the constraint. This repo's `CLAUDE.md`
31:    carries a quiet-period rule covering `-Prepare` to wrapper exit only,
32:    and says in its own text that no prose rule in `skills/` carries it.
33:    The plugin therefore does not take the rule to the repos it is
34:    installed into.
35: 
36: ## What the reporting session proposed, and why it is not the fix
37: 
38: The report proposed fusing the mirror build and `-Prepare` into one
39: command. That fusion is CONSTRUCTIBLE: one script could build, read the
40: probe's reported override hash, generate the wrapper body from it, and
41: then prepare. An earlier draft of this document called it impossible,
42: which was wrong, and cross-vendor review of that draft said so.
43: 
44: It is refused on a different ground. It closes the first of three windows
45: and leaves the largest one open. A round that takes ten minutes still
46: voids if an ignored file moves while the reviewer reads. Buying the small
47: window at the cost of a more complex build, while the round-length window
48: stays exactly as it was, is not the trade this defect calls for.
49: 
50: The ordering the fusion would have to respect is real and worth writing
51: down, because it constrains any future attempt: the build runs the client
52: context probe, the probe writes the verified override file, and the
53: wrapper body embeds that file's SHA256
54: (`skills/multi-model-verify/SKILL.md`, the `verified-override-dispatch`
55: region). So the build must complete before the wrapper body can exist,
56: and the body must exist before `-Prepare` can install it.
57: 
58: The real constraint is a QUIET PERIOD over the reviewed repository, from
59: the build until the wrapper exits.
60: 
61: ## Decisions
62: 
63: **D1. The gate does not change.** No path is removed from the digest, no
64: carve-out is added, and no re-mint or reseal mode is introduced. A
65: volatile-path allowlist was considered and rejected for this cycle: the
66: directories that move on their own (`.claude/`, `.codex/`) are instruction
67: surfaces, and excluding an instruction surface from the tamper net defeats
68: the check. It is recorded as an open backlog item, not built.
69: 
70: **D2. The refusal explains itself, advisorily.** The build writes the
71: SOURCE side's content manifest to a SIBLING of the mirror directory,
72: `<MirrorPath>.source-manifest`. On the source-status refusal path only,
73: `-VerifyIdentity` reads that file and names the paths that appeared,
74: vanished, or changed content.
75: 
76: The advisory file is safe by construction and must stay that way:
77: 
78: - It is read ONLY after the comparison has already decided to block, so
79:   it can never turn a refusal into a pass. A missing, unreadable,
80:   truncated, or deliberately corrupted file changes the printed
81:   explanation and never the exit code.
82: - It is a sibling, not a child, of the mirror and lives outside the
83:   repository, so it enters neither `source_status_sha256` nor
84:   `mirror_state_sha256`.
85: - It pins nothing, which is why it may be a file at all. The header's
86:   rule that identity values are passed as arguments rather than re-read
87:   from a file the build wrote applies to values that PIN something; this
88:   one carries no authority.
89: 
90: **D2a. Its DESTINATION carries the same guards `-OverrideOut` already
91: carries.** Cross-vendor review of the first draft found that writing to a
92: derived path with no guard is itself a write into an unknown file: a hard
93: link or symbolic link at that path would carry the write through to its
94: target, and a target inside the repository would be corrupted by the very
95: function that exists to explain a corrupted repository. The same review
96: found that an explicitly supplied `-OverrideOut <MirrorPath>.source-
97: manifest` passes today's checks and would then be overwritten, breaking
98: the wrapper's override hash.
99: 
100: The default override path is already a sibling of exactly this shape,
101: `<MirrorPath>.skills-override.txt` (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:956-973`),
102: and it is guarded for overlap with the repository and the mirror, for
103: pre-existence, and for the path budget. The sidecar takes the identical
104: guard set, plus an overlap check against the override path itself, plus a
105: refusal of a root-shaped mirror path such as `D:\`, which would otherwise
106: derive the drive-relative `D:.source-manifest`. The write itself uses
107: create-new semantics so that a file appearing between the guard and the
108: write cannot be clobbered either.
109: 
110: **D2b. The explanation states only what the comparison measures.** The
111: same review found three overstatements in the first draft, and the
112: corrected wording is part of this design rather than a detail of it:
113: 
114: - Manifest membership is not file existence. A deletion-only entry is
115:   omitted from the manifest by `Get-ManifestSubject`
116:   (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:460-463`), and a clean tracked file that
117:   becomes dirty ENTERS it. So the groups are named `entered coverage` and
118:   `left coverage`, never `appeared` and `vanished`.
119: - No content difference does not prove the status listing changed. A
120:   replaced sidecar produces the same empty result. The fallback says the
121:   advisory manifest did not identify the cause, and stops there.
122: - A malformed record is reported, never silently dropped, because a
123:   dropped record is a difference the explanation would then fail to
124:   mention.
125: 
126: **D3. The quiet period ships in the skill.** A contract region states it,
127: covering build through wrapper exit, so it travels with the plugin.
128: 
129: The region states the operational rule AND its measurement limits,
130: because a contract region that claims more than its mechanism delivers is
131: the defect class this whole cycle is about. Three limits, all found by
132: cross-vendor review of the first draft:
133: 
134: - The check samples endpoints. A change made and reverted inside the
135:   round is not detected, and no before-and-after check could detect it
136:   (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:305-307` says so already).
137: - A tracked file that git reports CLEAN is covered by neither
138:   fingerprint, so a raw-byte change surviving the clean filter is not
139:   covered (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:726-731`).
140: - Only the THIRD comparison costs a reviewer round. The preparation check
141:   and the wrapper's pre-client check both run before the client is
142:   invoked, so tripping either wastes no quota. The first draft said a
143:   round that trips the rule always spends its quota for nothing, which is
144:   true only of the post-client check.
145: 
146: **D4. The build is ordered last.** `references/preflight-mirror.md` gains
147: a timing rule: every act that writes inside the reviewed repository
148: finishes before the mirror is built.
149: 
150: ## Out of scope
151: 
152: - Any change to the digest's coverage (see D1).
153: - Item 91's cost question (the digest hashing a linked reference checkout
154:   repeatedly). Unrelated, already filed.
155: - Item 76's question about `.claude/skills` being materialised into the
156:   mirror. Unrelated, already filed.
157: 
158: ## Success criteria
159: 
160: - An ignored-file edit between build and verify produces a refusal that
161:   NAMES the file, on both PowerShell hosts.
162: - Deleting or corrupting the advisory manifest changes no exit code in
163:   any case, proven by test.
164: - `test_contract_coverage.py` reports the new region locked.
165: - All six CI tiers and both PowerShell hosts pass.

39: ### What the voided reply changed, before any countable round
40: 
41: Accepted and applied to the plan and the design:
42: 
43: 1. "Fusing build and prepare is not constructible" was WRONG. The fusion
44:    is constructible; it simply does not close the round-length window.
45:    The design now says that.
46: 2. The sidecar's destination had NO guards. A hard link or symbolic link
47:    at the derived path would carry the write to its target, and an
48:    explicitly supplied `-OverrideOut <MirrorPath>.source-manifest` would
49:    be silently overwritten, breaking the wrapper's override hash. The
50:    sidecar now takes the override's whole guard set plus create-new
51:    semantics.
52: 3. A root-shaped mirror path such as `D:\` derived the drive-relative
53:    `D:.source-manifest` rather than a sibling. Now refused.
54: 4. PowerShell hashtables compare keys case-insensitively, so a case-only
55:    rename would have reported no drift while the digest changed. Now
56:    ordinal dictionaries.
57: 5. `appeared` and `vanished` overstated what manifest membership means.
58:    Now `entered manifest coverage` and `left manifest coverage`.
59: 6. "No content difference, so the status listing itself changed" was an
60:    unsupported inference: a replaced sidecar produces the same result.
61:    Now says the manifest did not identify the cause.
62: 7. Malformed records were silently dropped. Now counted and reported.
63: 8. "Returns no value a caller could branch on" misstated PowerShell:
64:    `Write-Output` emits into the pipeline. The real protection is that
65:    the caller ignores it and exits unconditionally.
66: 9. "Never throws" covered only the read. The whole body is now wrapped.
67: 10. The quiet-period contract text claimed every trip spends quota. Only
68:     the post-client check does. It now states that and its two
69:     measurement limits.
70: 11. Unbounded read and unescaped names in the printed explanation. Now
71:     bounded, with control characters rendered.
72: 12. The reference told the operator to rebuild without reading the
73:     explanation first, and gave no route to `mirror.verify` for the
74:     wrapper's own refusals. Both fixed.

76: Pre-existing findings outside this plan's range are recorded as backlog
77: follow-ups rather than fixed here, per the debate protocol's scope rule.

24: 
25: **S1. Class sweep**
26: 
27: The following instances are evidenced beyond the numbered verdicts:
28: 
29: - **The diagnostic makes an unsupported causal inference.** A sidecar replaced with the live manifest produces no reported content differences even when content drift caused the refusal. The message then asserts that the status listing itself changed. A mutable advisory comparison cannot establish that conclusion. Malformed records are also silently dropped, and duplicate keys overwrite earlier records. Replace the conclusion with “the advisory manifest did not identify the cause,” and report malformed input as unavailable. ([plan:274](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:274), [plan:281](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:281), [plan:353](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:353).)
30: 
31: - **Manifest membership does not establish file appearance or disappearance.** Editing a previously clean tracked file adds it to this manifest; restoring a dirty tracked file to HEAD removes it. Both files can exist throughout. The proposed `appeared` and `vanished` labels therefore overstate what the comparison measures. Label these as entering or leaving manifest coverage, or obtain separate evidence of existence. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:437`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:473`; [plan:292](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:292).)
32: 
33: - **Case-only pathname changes disappear from the diagnostic.** The proposed PowerShell hashtables compare string keys without case sensitivity. In-memory execution on both hosts returned empty drift groups for `File.txt` versus `file.txt` with identical hashes, although the digest incorporates the original strings. Use ordinal key comparison when explaining the digest’s inputs. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:655`; [plan:277](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:277), [plan:283](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:283).)
34: 
35: - **“Returns nothing” misstates PowerShell output semantics.** `Write-Output` emits values a caller can capture and branch on. The relevant protection is that this caller ignores those values and exits unconditionally. Also, the function’s catch encloses only reading; it does not establish the advertised “never throws” guarantee for parsing and output. ([plan:316](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:316), [plan:320](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:320), [plan:333](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:333), [plan:349](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:349).)
36: 
37: - **The quiet-period prose overstates enforcement and quota consumption.** The mechanism samples state, misses reverted mutations, and expressly excludes raw-byte changes that Git still reports clean. Preparation and pre-client refusals occur before the client invocation, so those failures do not inherently spend a reviewer round. Keep the conservative operational rule while stating these measurement limits. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:727`; `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:286`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:291`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:305`; [plan:416](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:416).)
38: 
39: - **“Never writes to the real tree” contradicts the tool’s own account of Git status.** The header makes the absolute promise, while the directory-link guard explains that status captures write the source repository’s index. Qualify the promise or suppress those optional writes. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:19`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1101`.)
40: 
41: - **Construction comments claim more than endpoint comparisons prove.** The comment says the retained value distinguishes a source that moved and moved back. Equality at the observed endpoints cannot do so. The existing identity contract already acknowledges that intermediate bytes can reach the copy despite matching endpoints. Align the comments with that contract. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1308`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1382`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1416`; `skills/multi-model-verify/references/backup-lane.md:723`.)
42: 
43: - **Verification’s same-directory protection checks spelling rather than filesystem identity.** It compares provider-resolved strings, then proceeds without the build’s reparse-point checks. A junction alias of a source directory can have a different spelling while reaching the same files. The claimed same-directory refusal therefore exceeds this comparison. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:734`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:759`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:787`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1258`.)
44: 
45: - **Extra-input coverage can fail silently.** The tool promises to copy and record each extra input, but `Copy-Item` has neither terminating error handling nor a success check. A nonterminating copy failure can leave an input absent; the subsequent manifest cannot discover an absent file that never entered status. Make failed copies explicit refusals. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:39`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:519`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1554`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1684`.)
46: 
47: - **Generated wrappers do not preserve arbitrary accepted path text.** Paths are embedded in the wrapper, which is then written as ASCII. An accepted path containing `é` becomes `?`; the in-memory encoding probe confirmed this. Use an encoding readable without loss on both hosts. Separately, the printed command places paths in expandable double-quoted strings without escaping `$` or backticks; a legal path component such as `$(1+1)` becomes executable expression syntax. (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:341`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:570`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:591`.)
48: 
49: - **Classification redemption is not exclusive.** Reading and comparing `classifying:<nonce>` does not reserve ownership atomically. Two callers can both pass that comparison before either writes the final state. The “this call owns the reservation” assertion needs exclusive access or a narrower guarantee. (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:50`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:663`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:675`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:682`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:644`.)
50: 
51: - **Dispatch failure guarantees exceed the error paths.** A receipt write failure after creation can leave a partial receipt, contradicting “no receipt.” Wrapper verification failures throw before classification, so their exit cannot carry the promised classification state. An oversized integer in `exit` passes the regex and then throws during the unchecked integer conversion instead of following the stated classification mapping. (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:121`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:572`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:580`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:287`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:311`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:790`.)
52: 
53: **S2. Other forms**
54: 
55: - **Destructive or disruptive writes:** besides the alias case in claims 5–6, an explicitly supplied `-OverrideOut <MirrorPath>.source-manifest` passes the existing separation checks. The probe writes its verified override there; the proposed sidecar writer then overwrites it. The documented wrapper consequently refuses its hash. Skip advisory creation on collisions without touching the existing artifact. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:960`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:1725`; `skills/multi-model-verify/SKILL.md:184`; [plan:114](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:114), [plan:124](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:124).)
56: 
57: - **Misleading the operator:** forged valid records can blame harmless files or suppress the real content change. The output contains no advisory warning beside the claims, and its fallback positively attributes the cause to status metadata. The reference’s “Build again” advice can therefore encourage replacing evidence before investigating the actual change. Put the uncertainty in the emitted explanation itself. ([plan:281](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:281), [plan:342](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:342), [plan:354](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:354), [plan:537](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:537).)
58: 
59: - **Resource exhaustion and output injection:** `ReadAllLines` reads an unbounded file, the parser stores and sorts unbounded records, and the twenty-name limit leaves each printed name unbounded. Sidecar names bypass `Test-SupportedPathname`, allowing control characters and terminal escapes into output. Bound input bytes, record lengths, and total output; escape control characters. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:254`; [plan:279](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:279), [plan:302](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:302), [plan:322](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:322), [plan:345](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:345).)
60: 
61: - **Root-path construction:** the proposed helper maps `D:\` to `D:.source-manifest`, a drive-relative path rather than a sibling. The in-memory probe confirmed that result. Reject root-shaped inputs for advisory placement or derive a separately validated destination. ([plan:102](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:102).)
62: 
63: - **Encoding corruption:** a first pathname beginning with U+FEFF is accepted by the pathname guard. The BOM-free writer emits those initial bytes as pathname content, but the BOM-detecting reader consumes them as an encoding marker. The in-memory stream-reader probe reproduced that character loss. Use explicit byte decoding that preserves pathname data. (`tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:249`, `tools/new-review-mirror.ps1:254`; [plan:113](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:113), [plan:322](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:322).)
64: 
65: - **Diagnostics remain hidden during wrapper failures:** both wrapper verifications redirect their details into `mirror.verify`, then throw a generic message. The proposed reference only describes “lines beneath” the refusal and gives no route to that file. Document the log location or surface a bounded explanation through the wrapper. (`tools/dispatch-round.ps1:286`, `tools/dispatch-round.ps1:310`; [plan:542](C:/Temp/pxr1/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-05-mirror-identity-window.md:542).)

 succeeded in 209ms:
1: # new-review-mirror.ps1 - build the throwaway review mirror, perform
2: # SKILL.md preflight-3 remediation inside it, and print the evidence the
3: # debate record needs.
4: #
5: # The mirror is a FILE COPY that preserves .git, never a git clone: a clone
6: # carries TRACKED FILES ONLY and the review inputs are routinely gitignored
7: # (frozen plans, References/). Probed 2026-07-26 in KitnEssentials, where a
8: # cloned workspace handed the reviewer a tree with nothing to review while
9: # every route and containment check stayed green.
10: #
11: # Directory links are NOT copied through. robocopy runs with /XJD and the
12: # tool re-creates each link as a junction after the last sweep, so the
13: # reviewer reads the target through the same relative path while the
14: # mirror carries none of its bytes. Measured 2026-09-05: one addon's
15: # reference link held 14,884 files, doubling every mirror. The identity
16: # digests still cover that content, because the manifest expands a
17: # directory subject through a link on both hosts.
18: #
19: # This script never writes to the real tree, never dispatches a review, and
20: # never decides to proceed. It stops immediately before the brief is
21: # written, because the brief is the first artifact that is not evidence.
22: #
23: # Windows PowerShell 5.1 compatible, ASCII ONLY.
24: #
25: # Exit codes: 0 built and clean, 1 blocked (reason on stdout), 2 script or
26: # environment error.
27: # TWO MODES. Build constructs the mirror and prints the record. Verify
28: # re-checks that record against the live source and the live mirror
29: # immediately before a dispatch, which is step 6 of the construction
30: # bridge: the two-HEAD gate is worthless if it is only ever evaluated at
31: # the moment the mirror is made.
32: #
33: #   Build:  -RepoRoot <src> -MirrorPath <dst> [-OverrideOut <p>] [-Force]
34: #           [-SkipProbe] [-CodexCommand <exe>] [-ExtraInput <path>]...
35: #   Verify: -VerifyIdentity -RepoRoot <src> -MirrorPath <dst>
36: #           -ExpectedMirrorPath <path> -SourceHead <sha> -MirrorHead <sha>
37: #           -SourceStatusSha256 <hex> -MirrorStateSha256 <hex>
38: #
39: # -ExtraInput names a file the mirror cannot inherit on its own - a
40: # standards file living above the repo root, a spec kept outside the
41: # tree - and may be repeated. Each one is copied into the mirror BEFORE
42: # the baseline and the manifest are taken, so the identity record covers
43: # it and the printed record enumerates it like any other mirror content.
44: # There is deliberately no way to add one after construction, and no
45: # re-mint or reseal mode: a tree that changed since it was measured is
46: # exactly what -VerifyIdentity exists to refuse, so re-blessing one
47: # would defeat it.
48: #
49: # The identity values are passed as ARGUMENTS rather than re-read from a
50: # file the build wrote, for the reason the codex brief binding states
51: # about its own expected hash: a file re-read later is mutable and would
52: # silently redefine the value it is supposed to pin.
53: param(
54:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Verify", Mandatory = $true)]
55:     [switch]$VerifyIdentity,
56:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Verify", Mandatory = $true)]
57:     [AllowEmptyString()][string]$SourceHead,
58:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Verify", Mandatory = $true)]
59:     [AllowEmptyString()][string]$MirrorHead,
60:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Verify", Mandatory = $true)]
61:     [AllowEmptyString()][string]$SourceStatusSha256,
62:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Verify", Mandatory = $true)]
63:     [AllowEmptyString()][string]$MirrorStateSha256,

70:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Build", Mandatory = $true)]
71:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Verify", Mandatory = $true)]

75:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Build")][switch]$Force,
76:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Build")][switch]$SkipProbe,
77:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Build")][string]$CodexCommand = "codex",
78:     # Catches every remaining token so the BUILD can accept a repeated
79:     # named flag. PowerShell's own parameter binder refuses to bind the
80:     # SAME named parameter twice, even to an array - "Cannot bind
81:     # parameter because parameter 'X' is specified more than once" -
82:     # which is what a literal `-ExtraInput <path>` array parameter would
83:     # do. -ExtraInput is parsed out of this list by hand below, and
84:     # anything left over is a parameter this tool does not have.
85:     [Parameter(ParameterSetName = "Build", ValueFromRemainingArguments = $true)]
86:     [string[]]$BuildRemainingArgs

89: # This script decodes git's pathnames as STRICT UTF-8 and refuses to guess
90: # on a bad byte, then prints them. Without this line the print undoes that
91: # care: [Console]::OutputEncoding defaults to the OEM code page - measured
92: # IBM437 on both hosts here - so an accented pathname leaves as one 0x82
93: # byte and a UTF-8 reader sees U+FFFD. The baseline and the manifest are
94: # EVIDENCE, and evidence that renames a file is worse than no evidence.
95: # The ambient encoding also varies by console, which is why this surfaced
96: # as an order-dependent test rather than a constant one.
97: [Console]::OutputEncoding = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)
98: 
99: function Invoke-GitProcess($repo, $gitArgs) {
100:     # Run git and hand back its stdout as RAW BYTES.
101:     #
102:     # Reading bytes rather than PowerShell's decoded string is the point.
103:     # Windows PowerShell 5.1 decodes a native command's output with the
104:     # console code page, and even a UTF-8 console override maps a malformed
105:     # byte to U+FFFD SILENTLY. On this boundary a substituted character is
106:     # a wrong pathname reported as a good one, so the decode happens once,
107:     # downstream, and strictly.
108:     #
109:     # ARGUMENTS ARE WHITESPACE-FREE LITERALS AND THE REPO IS THE WORKING
110:     # DIRECTORY. .NET Framework's ProcessStartInfo has no ArgumentList, so
111:     # the command line is one string; passing the repo path through it
112:     # would mean hand-quoting a value that can contain spaces, which is the
113:     # defect class this change removes. The guard below refuses an argument
114:     # this command line cannot express rather than trusting the caller.
115:     #
116:     # Every argument is QUOTED. Git for Windows runs on the MSYS2 runtime,
117:     # which glob-expands an unquoted argument, and `*AGENTS.md` must reach
118:     # git unexpanded or the back-channel enumeration silently narrows to
119:     # whatever happens to sit in the working directory.
120:     foreach ($a in @($gitArgs)) {
121:         if ([string]$a -match '[\s"]') {
122:             return @{ Ok = $false; Bytes = @()
123:                       Reason = ("git argument '" + $a + "' carries" +
124:                         " whitespace or a quote, which this command line" +
125:                         " cannot express") }
126:         }
127:     }
128:     $quoted = @($gitArgs | ForEach-Object { '"' + $_ + '"' })
129:     $psi = New-Object System.Diagnostics.ProcessStartInfo
130:     $psi.FileName = "git"
131:     $psi.Arguments = ($quoted -join " ")
132:     $psi.WorkingDirectory = $repo
133:     $psi.UseShellExecute = $false
134:     $psi.CreateNoWindow = $true
135:     $psi.RedirectStandardOutput = $true
136:     $psi.RedirectStandardError = $true
137:     $mem = New-Object System.IO.MemoryStream
138:     $p = $null
139:     try {
140:         $p = [System.Diagnostics.Process]::Start($psi)
141:         # Drain stderr CONCURRENTLY. A full stderr pipe blocks git before
142:         # it finishes writing stdout, and the stdout read below would then
143:         # never return.
144:         $errTask = $p.StandardError.ReadToEndAsync()
145:         $p.StandardOutput.BaseStream.CopyTo($mem)
146:         $p.WaitForExit()
147:         [void]$errTask.Wait(5000)
148:     } catch {
149:         return @{ Ok = $false; Bytes = @()
150:                   Reason = ("git could not be run: " +
151:                             $_.Exception.Message) }
152:     }
153:     if ($p.ExitCode -ne 0) {
154:         return @{ Ok = $false; Bytes = @()
155:                   Reason = ("git exited " + $p.ExitCode) }
156:     }
157:     return @{ Ok = $true; Bytes = $mem.ToArray() }
158: }
159: 
160: function ConvertFrom-NulCapture($bytes) {
161:     # Turn a `-z` capture into its fields. Every failure here is a STOP.
162:     #
163:     # `-z` is why this exists. Git C-style-quotes a pathname whenever
164:     # quoting would change it, and the trigger set is WIDER than the
165:     # escapes alone: a plain SPACE is enough. Measured 2026-07-29, a
166:     # directory named `M+ Timer` came back from `status --porcelain` as
167:     # `?? "M+ Timer/"`, and `core.quotepath=false` did not suppress it.
168:     # Under `-z` git emits the pathname verbatim and NUL-terminated and
169:     # never quotes, so there is no escape grammar to decode.
170:     $raw = [byte[]]@($bytes)
171:     if ($raw.Length -eq 0) { return @{ Ok = $true; Fields = @() } }
172:     if ($raw[$raw.Length - 1] -ne 0) {
173:         return @{ Ok = $false; Fields = @()
174:                   Reason = ("the -z capture does not end with a NUL, so it" +
175:                     " was truncated and its last pathname is a fragment") }
176:     }
177:     # throwOnInvalidBytes, deliberately. The permissive decode substitutes
178:     # U+FFFD and hands back a pathname that names a different file.
179:     $utf8 = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false, $true)
180:     $text = ""
181:     try {
182:         $text = $utf8.GetString($raw)
183:     } catch {
184:         return @{ Ok = $false; Fields = @()
185:                   Reason = ("the -z capture is not valid UTF-8, so a" +
186:                     " pathname in it cannot be read without guessing") }
187:     }
188:     # The capture ends with a NUL, so the split's LAST element is the empty
189:     # string after it. Drop exactly that one. An empty field anywhere else
190:     # is a real fault and belongs to the caller, not to this function.
191:     $parts = $text.Split([char]0)
192:     $fields = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
193:     for ($i = 0; $i -lt $parts.Length - 1; $i++) {
194:         [void]$fields.Add($parts[$i])
195:     }
196:     return @{ Ok = $true; Fields = @($fields) }
197: }
198: 
199: function Test-SupportedPathname($value) {
200:     # Under `-z` git never quotes, so whatever arrives IS the pathname.
201:     # The question this answers is whether this tool can handle that
202:     # pathname EXACTLY, in both senses:
203:     #
204:     #   1. Would resolving it risk naming the WRONG file? This is NOT a
205:     #      Windows name validator: it does not check reserved device
206:     #      names, trailing dots, length limits, or the other characters
207:     #      Windows refuses. A syntactically fine name with no file
208:     #      behind it is not this function's business; where it lands
209:     #      depends on its status shape, and only ONE of those routes is
210:     #      a stop. See the design record - the manifest stops on a
211:     #      subject with no file, deletion-only entries are omitted
212:     #      before that, and a back-channel match goes to remediation.
213:     #   2. Can Format-StatusPathname record it in the porcelain line form
214:     #      the baseline contract already uses?
215:     #
216:     # The admitted set is therefore an ordinary pathname, or one whose ONLY
217:     # line-form quoting trigger is a SPACE - the one trigger the renderer
218:     # reproduces. Everything else the line form quotes or escapes some
219:     # other way is refused, and so is `>`.
220:     #
221:     # REFUSING rather than rendering is the deliberate choice for that
222:     # residue. Git records it with git's own C-style encoder - NAMED
223:     # escapes for tab, newline, `"` and `\`, and OCTAL for the rest,
224:     # including the one reachable case, 0x7F as `\177`. Reproducing any
225:     # of that would mean writing back the encoder this cycle deletes. A
226:     # loud stop on a pathological name is the cheaper failure.
227:     #
228:     # The BACKSLASH refusal carries a second, heavier reason. git separates
229:     # path components with a forward slash, so a backslash in a field is a
230:     # literal character in a NAME - and `Join-Path` would then read it as a
231:     # separator and resolve a DIFFERENT file, which the script would delete
232:     # or hash under the name the baseline gave. That is false coverage
233:     # rather than a refusal, which is the one outcome this preflight exists
234:     # to prevent.
235:     #
236:     # `>` is refused for a reason of its own: Format-StatusRecord renders a
237:     # rename as `<old> -> <new>`, and that arrow is only unambiguous while
238:     # no pathname can contain `>`. The guard is what makes that true rather
239:     # than assumed.
240:     #
241:     # 0x7F is refused, and it is the case that shows why this function is
242:     # NOT named for Windows. Measured 2026-07-29: Windows CREATES
243:     # `a<0x7F>b.txt`, and `git status --porcelain` prints it as
244:     # `?? "a\177b.txt"` with AND without `core.quotepath=false`, while `-z`
245:     # returns the byte raw. It is legal on disk and a quoting trigger this
246:     # renderer cannot reproduce, so admitting it would put a bare name into
247:     # a baseline the direct capture quotes.
248:     #
249:     # Non-ASCII is NOT refused. The old captures set `core.quotepath=false`
250:     # and recorded an accented pathname raw, so it is not a trigger for the
251:     # form this baseline uses.
252:     $text = [string]$value
253:     if ($text.Length -eq 0) { return $false }
254:     foreach ($ch in $text.ToCharArray()) {
255:         if ([int]$ch -lt 32) { return $false }
256:         if ([int]$ch -eq 127) { return $false }
257:         if ($ch -eq [char]34) { return $false }
258:         if ($ch -eq '\') { return $false }
259:         if ($ch -eq '>') { return $false }
260:     }
261:     return $true
262: }
263: 
264: function ConvertTo-StatusRecord($fields) {
265:     # Parse the `-z` status capture STRUCTURALLY. Returns @{Records=..} or
266:     # @{Error=..}; a caller that cannot parse an entry must BLOCK, never
267:     # skip, because a skipped entry is a silent hole in the manifest.
268:     #
269:     # THE RENAME FIELD ORDER IS INVERTED FROM THE LINE FORM. Measured
270:     # 2026-07-29: the line form reads `R  <old> -> <new>`, and the same
271:     # rename under `-z` reads `R  <new>` NUL `<old>` NUL - DESTINATION
272:     # first, SOURCE second. Nothing about the record hints at the order,
273:     # which is why it is stated here rather than left to a reader.
274:     #
275:     # Each field is the two status columns, one space, then the pathname.
276:     $records = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
277:     $all = @($fields)
278:     $i = 0
279:     while ($i -lt $all.Count) {
280:         $field = [string]$all[$i]
281:         $i++
282:         if ($field.Length -lt 4) {
283:             return @{ Error = ("status field '" + $field + "' is shorter" +
284:                 " than two status columns, a space and a pathname") }
285:         }
286:         if ($field[2] -ne ' ') {
287:             return @{ Error = ("status field '" + $field + "' has no space" +
288:                 " where the status code ends, so this capture is not the" +
289:                 " porcelain format this parser reads") }
290:         }
291:         $x = $field[0]
292:         $y = $field[1]
293:         $path = $field.Substring(3)
294:         if (-not (Test-SupportedPathname $path)) {
295:             return @{ Error = ("status entry '" + $field + "' names a" +
296:                 " path this script cannot handle exactly, on one of the" +
297:                 " guard's two grounds: resolving it would risk naming the" +
298:                 " WRONG file, or it cannot be recorded unambiguously in" +
299:                 " the baseline's line form") }
300:         }
301:         $source = $null
302:         if ($x -eq "R" -or $x -eq "C" -or $y -eq "R" -or $y -eq "C") {
303:             if ($i -ge $all.Count) {
304:                 return @{ Error = ("rename or copy entry '" + $field +
305:                     "' has no source field after it, so the capture is" +
306:                     " truncated") }
307:             }
308:             $source = [string]$all[$i]
309:             $i++
310:             if (-not (Test-SupportedPathname $source)) {
311:                 return @{ Error = ("rename or copy entry '" + $field +
312:                     "' names a source this script cannot handle" +
313:                     " exactly, on the same two grounds") }
314:             }
315:         }
316:         [void]$records.Add(@{ X = $x; Y = $y; Path = $path
317:                               Source = $source })
318:     }
319:     return @{ Records = @($records) }
320: }
321: 
322: function Format-StatusPathname($path) {
323:     # Reproduce the porcelain LINE form's quoting for one pathname, so a
324:     # recorded baseline is byte-comparable with a capture taken by running
325:     # THE STATUS COMMAND directly - which is what
326:     # references/backup-lane.md requires of every round.
327:     #
328:     # The rule is one condition because the guard has already removed every
329:     # other trigger. Measured 2026-07-29: `git status --porcelain` quotes
330:     # each pathname independently and, once `"` , `\` and the control
331:     # characters are refused, a SPACE is the only remaining trigger. In the
332:     # same measurement a rename with a spaced source and a plain
333:     # destination printed `R  "with space/a.lua" -> plain/a.lua`, and the
334:     # reverse printed `R  plain/b.lua -> "with space/b.lua"` - each side on
335:     # its own.
336:     #
337:     # Non-ASCII is deliberately NOT quoted. The old captures set
338:     # `core.quotepath=false`, so an accented pathname was recorded raw, and
339:     # this render must keep that shape rather than introduce quoting the
340:     # baseline never had.
341:     if ([string]$path -match ' ') { return ('"' + $path + '"') }
342:     return [string]$path
343: }
344: 
345: function Format-StatusRecord($record) {
346:     # Render one record in git's own DISPLAY order, `R  <old> -> <new>`, so
347:     # the recorded baseline keeps the shape references/backup-lane.md
348:     # already describes and two captures stay comparable across this
349:     # change.
350:     #
351:     # ONE WAY ONLY. Nothing re-parses this text: the manifest's subjects
352:     # come from the RECORDS. That separation is the whole reason the arrow
353:     # is safe to write here at all, and Test-SupportedPathname refusing `>`
354:     # is what keeps it unambiguous.
355:     $head = [string]$record.X + [string]$record.Y + " "
356:     if ($record.Source) {
357:         return ($head + (Format-StatusPathname $record.Source) + " -> " +
358:                 (Format-StatusPathname $record.Path))
359:     }
360:     return ($head + (Format-StatusPathname $record.Path))
361: }
362: 
363: function Invoke-GitFields($repo, $gitArgs) {
364:     # Every git capture that produces PATHNAMES goes through here, so both
365:     # of them answer the same questions the same way.
366:     #
367:     # `core.quotepath=false` is GONE from these captures and its absence is
368:     # deliberate. The flag governed the DISPLAY form, and `-z` has no
369:     # display form: measured 2026-07-29, `git ls-files -z` returned raw
370:     # UTF-8 bytes with the flag ABSENT. Keeping a flag that does nothing
371:     # invites a later reader to treat it as load-bearing.
372:     #
373:     # The console-encoding guard the old capture needed is gone for the
374:     # same reason: nothing here goes through PowerShell's decoder. The
375:     # bytes are read from the process stream and decoded once, strictly,
376:     # in ConvertFrom-NulCapture.
377:     $captured = Invoke-GitProcess $repo $gitArgs
378:     if (-not $captured.Ok) {
379:         return @{ Ok = $false; Fields = @(); Reason = $captured.Reason }
380:     }
381:     return (ConvertFrom-NulCapture $captured.Bytes)
382: }
383: 
384: function Get-BackChannelEntry($repo) {
385:     # One listing covering tracked, untracked AND ignored files. `--others`
386:     # without `--exclude-standard` includes ignored paths. `*AGENTS.md`
387:     # reaches any depth; `.agents/*` is anchored at the repo ROOT and does
388:     # NOT, which is the asymmetry recorded in SKILL.md's
389:     # enumeration-depth-asymmetry region. Do not restate "at any depth"
390:     # here.
391:     #
392:     # Returns @{Ok=..; Entries=..; Reason=..}. A function returning a bare
393:     # @() has its empty array unrolled by PowerShell, so the caller's
394:     # variable becomes $null and a CLEAN repo reads exactly like a FAILED
395:     # enumeration.
396:     $r = Invoke-GitFields $repo @("ls-files", "--cached", "--others", "-z",
397:                                   "*AGENTS.md", ".agents/*", '.kimi-code/*')
398:     if (-not $r.Ok) {
399:         return @{ Ok = $false; Entries = @(); Reason = $r.Reason }
400:     }
401:     # An entry this script cannot handle exactly is a stop, on either of
402:     # the guard's two grounds - a name whose resolution would risk the
403:     # WRONG file, or a name it could resolve but could not record.
404:     # Reporting either as clean is the one outcome the whole preflight
405:     # exists to prevent, and a back-channel is the case where that matters
406:     # most.
407:     foreach ($e in @($r.Fields)) {
408:         if (-not (Test-SupportedPathname $e)) {
409:             return @{ Ok = $false; Entries = @()
410:                       Reason = ("the back-channel entry '" + $e + "' names" +
411:                         " a path this script cannot handle exactly, so it" +
412:                         " cannot be enumerated, deleted and recorded as a" +
413:                         " single consistent fact") }
414:         }
415:     }
416:     return @{ Ok = $true; Entries = @($r.Fields) }
417: }
418: 
419: function Test-Tracked($repo, $path) {
420:     & git -C $repo ls-files --error-unmatch -- $path 2>$null | Out-Null
421:     return ($LASTEXITCODE -eq 0)
422: }
423: 
424: function Get-BaselineRaw($repo) {
425:     # THE STATUS COMMAND, every capture without exception. Bare porcelain
426:     # OMITS ignored paths and COLLAPSES an untracked directory to one
427:     # entry; ignored content is the entire reason this workspace is a
428:     # mirror.
429:     #
430:     # The BASELINE is this raw capture, status codes included
431:     # (references/backup-lane.md). It is recorded verbatim and is NOT the
432:     # same object as the manifest's subject list below.
433:     #
434:     # Same structured shape and same reason as Get-BackChannelEntry: an
435:     # empty baseline is a legitimate state, and a bare @() return would be
436:     # indistinguishable from a failed capture.
437:     return (Invoke-GitFields $repo @("status", "--porcelain", "--ignored",
438:                                      "-uall", "-z"))
439: }
440: 
441: function Get-ManifestSubject($records) {
442:     # Coverage is exactly the baseline's paths. Returns @{Paths=..} or
443:     # @{Error=..}.
444:     #
445:     # Two dispositions are DEFINED and are not the same thing as skipping.
446:     # A deletion-only entry is OMITTED, deliberately, because it has no
447:     # bytes; an `RD` destination is a STOP. What must never happen is a
448:     # third thing: an entry passed over because this function could not
449:     # make sense of it. That is the silent hole in the manifest, and it is
450:     # what the stops below exist to prevent.
451:     #
452:     # Takes RECORDS, never rendered text. A rename's destination is already
453:     # the record's Path under `-z`, so there is no arrow to search for and
454:     # no chance of splitting inside a pathname.
455:     $paths = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
456:     foreach ($r in @($records)) {
457:         $x = $r.X
458:         $y = $r.Y
459:         # Deletion-only entries have no bytes to hash. HEAD plus the
460:         # baseline already bind the absence, which is the whole content of
461:         # the fact, so OMIT them.
462:         if (($x -eq " " -and $y -eq "D") -or ($x -eq "D" -and $y -eq " ")) {
463:             continue
464:         }
465:         # An `RD` destination no longer exists. That entry names no
466:         # readable file, so it is a stop rather than a silent omission.
467:         if ($y -eq "D" -and ($x -eq "R" -or $x -eq "C")) {
468:             return @{ Error = ("baseline entry '" +
469:                 (Format-StatusRecord $r) + "' names a destination that has" +
470:                 " been deleted; the mirror is not in a state this manifest" +
471:                 " rule can describe") }
472:         }
473:         [void]$paths.Add($r.Path)
474:     }
475:     return @{ Paths = @($paths) }
476: }
477: 
478: function Get-FilesBeneath($start, $visited, $depth) {
479:     # Every file beneath $start, INCLUDING files behind every directory
480:     # link found under it, as full paths. Measured 2026-09-05 on both
481:     # hosts: Get-ChildItem -Recurse enters a link only when the link IS
482:     # the start path; from a parent it lists the link and does not pass
483:     # through it. So each link beneath a subject gets a listing of its
484:     # own, started at the link. $visited holds every resolved link
485:     # target this expansion has entered, seeded by the caller with the
486:     # repository root; a repeat is a refusal. Cross-vendor round 2
487:     # (2026-09-05) showed the visited set alone is not a cycle guard: a
488:     # RELATIVE symbolic-link target resolved against the link's spelled
489:     # parent yields a new string on every level of a cycle reached
490:     # through an outer junction. $depth is the bound that closes that:
491:     # a link nested more than 16 links deep is refused. Returns
492:     # @{Paths=..} or @{Error=..}.
493:     if ($depth -gt 16) {
494:         return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' sits more than 16 directory" +
495:             " links deep; the manifest refuses a link graph that deep" +
496:             " because a relative-link cycle presents exactly like it") }
497:     }
498:     $out = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
499:     $startAttr = 0
500:     try {
501:         $startAttr = [int][System.IO.File]::GetAttributes($start)
502:     } catch {
503:         return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' could not be read: " +
504:                            $_.Exception.Message) }
505:     }
506:     if (($startAttr -band [int][System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint) -ne 0) {
507:         $target = $null
508:         try {
509:             $target = (Get-Item -LiteralPath $start -Force -ErrorAction Stop).Target
510:             if ($null -ne $target -and $target -isnot [string]) {
511:                 foreach ($candidate in $target) { $target = $candidate; break }
512:             }
513:         } catch { $target = $null }
514:         if ([string]::IsNullOrEmpty($target)) {
515:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' is a directory link whose" +
516:                                " target could not be read") }
517:         }
518:         $targetFull = ""
519:         # WRAPPED, because this script never sets $ErrorActionPreference
520:         # to Stop: a .NET exception here is NON-TERMINATING, so an
521:         # unwrapped GetFullPath left $targetFull empty, put the empty
522:         # string into the visited set, and returned Paths with no Error -
523:         # a failed measurement reading as a clean one. Measured by the
524:         # cross-vendor reviewer with a 270-character target
525:         # (PathTooLongException), 2026-09-05.
526:         try {
527:             if ([System.IO.Path]::IsPathRooted($target)) {
528:                 $targetFull = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($target)
529:             } else {
530:                 $targetFull = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath(
531:                     [System.IO.Path]::Combine(
532:                         [System.IO.Path]::GetDirectoryName(
533:                             [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($start)), $target))
534:             }
535:         } catch {
536:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' is a directory link whose" +
537:                 " target " + $target + " could not be resolved to a full" +
538:                 " path: " + $_.Exception.Message) }
539:         }
540:         if ($seamFailLinkTarget) {
541:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' link target resolution" +
542:                 " failed (test seam)") }
543:         }
544:         if (-not $visited.Add($targetFull.TrimEnd("\"))) {
545:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' reaches " + $targetFull +
546:                 " which this expansion already entered; the manifest refuses" +
547:                 " a link graph that repeats or cycles") }
548:         }
549:     }
550:     $found = $null
551:     try {
552:         $found = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $start -Recurse -File -Force `
553:             -ErrorAction Stop
554:     } catch {
555:         return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' could not be enumerated: " +
556:                            $_.Exception.Message) }
557:     }
558:     foreach ($f in $found) { [void]$out.Add($f.FullName) }
559:     $links = $null
560:     try {
561:         $links = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $start -Recurse -Directory -Force `
562:             -ErrorAction Stop | Where-Object {
563:                 ($_.Attributes -band [System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint) -ne 0 })
564:     } catch {
565:         return @{ Error = ("'" + $start + "' could not be enumerated for" +
566:                            " links: " + $_.Exception.Message) }
567:     }
568:     foreach ($l in $links) {
569:         $inner = Get-FilesBeneath $l.FullName $visited ($depth + 1)
570:         if ($inner.ContainsKey("Error")) { return $inner }
571:         foreach ($p in @($inner.Paths)) { [void]$out.Add($p) }
572:     }
573:     return @{ Paths = @($out) }
574: }
575: 
576: function Get-ContentManifest($repo, $paths) {
577:     # Directories expand RECURSIVELY to their files: a directory subject
578:     # such as References/ is never one manifest entry, because a hash over
579:     # a directory name identifies nothing.
580:     # A directory link beneath a subject is expanded by a listing started
581:     # at the link (Get-FilesBeneath), because a recursion from the parent
582:     # does not pass through it on either host.
583:     $files = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
584:     foreach ($p in $paths) {
585:         $full = Join-Path $repo $p
586:         if (Test-Path $full -PathType Container) {
587:             # -Stop, never the default: a swallowed enumeration error
588:             # omits every file under an unreadable subdirectory and the
589:             # manifest then reads as coverage of a tree it never saw.
590:             # Seeded with the repository root: a link back onto the root
591:             # is a cycle on its first step, not its second.
592:             $visited = New-Object "System.Collections.Generic.HashSet[string]" (
593:                 [System.StringComparer]::OrdinalIgnoreCase)
594:             [void]$visited.Add([System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($repo).TrimEnd("\"))
595:             $beneath = Get-FilesBeneath $full $visited 0
596:             if ($beneath.ContainsKey("Error")) {
597:                 return @{ Error = ("'" + $p + "': " + $beneath.Error) }
598:             }
599:             foreach ($fullName in @($beneath.Paths)) {
600:                 $rel = $fullName.Substring($repo.Length + 1)
601:                 [void]$files.Add($rel.Replace("\", "/"))
602:             }
603:         } elseif (Test-Path $full -PathType Leaf) {
604:             [void]$files.Add($p.TrimEnd("/").Replace("\", "/"))
605:         } else {
606:             # A baseline path with nothing behind it is a stop. Skipping it
607:             # would leave a hole in the manifest that reads as coverage.
608:             return @{ Error = "baseline path '$p' has no file behind it" }
609:         }
610:     }
611:     $unique = [string[]]@($files | Sort-Object -Unique)
612:     [Array]::Sort($unique, [System.StringComparer]::Ordinal)
613:     $sha = [System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()
614:     $out = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList
615:     foreach ($rel in $unique) {
616:         # A failed ReadAllBytes is NON-TERMINATING on both hosts
617:         # (measured 2026-08-04), so without this catch $bytes keeps the
618:         # PREVIOUS file's contents and this line records that hash for
619:         # the file it could not read - deterministically, so the wrong
620:         # value reproduces on every later run. Evidence that quietly
621:         # names the wrong bytes is worse than no evidence.
622:         $bytes = $null
623:         try {
624:             $bytes = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes((Join-Path $repo $rel))
625:         } catch {
626:             $sha.Dispose()
627:             return @{ Error = ("'" + $rel + "' could not be read: " +
628:                                $_.Exception.Message) }
629:         }
630:         $hex = ([System.BitConverter]::ToString($sha.ComputeHash($bytes)) -replace '-', '').ToLower()
631:         [void]$out.Add($rel + " " + $hex)
632:     }
633:     return @{ Paths = @($out) }
634: }
635: 
636: function Get-HeadSha($repo) {
637:     # @{Ok=$true; Sha=..} or @{Ok=$false; Reason=..}. Structured for the
638:     # same reason every other capture here is: a bare empty string cannot
639:     # be told apart from a failed read, and an unread HEAD must never
640:     # compare equal to anything.
641:     $out = (& git -C $repo rev-parse HEAD 2>$null | Out-String).Trim()
642:     if (($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) -or -not $out) {
643:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = "could not resolve HEAD in $repo" }
644:     }
645:     return @{ Ok = $true; Sha = $out }
646: }
647: 
648: function Get-CombinedSha256($fields, $manifestLines) {
649:     # ONE digest over a raw status capture's fields plus a content
650:     # manifest's lines, NUL-joined - the separator git already emits
651:     # them with, so no field value can forge a boundary. Shared by the
652:     # source-side fingerprint below and the mirror-side one at
653:     # construction and verify time, so the two are computed the same way
654:     # by construction rather than by two call sites staying in sync.
655:     $joined = ((@($fields) + @($manifestLines)) -join "`0")
656:     $bytes = [System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetBytes($joined)
657:     $sha = [System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::Create()
658:     try { $digest = $sha.ComputeHash($bytes) } finally { $sha.Dispose() }
659:     return (($digest | ForEach-Object { $_.ToString("x2") }) -join "")
660: }
661: 
662: function Get-StatusSha256($repo) {
663:     # A non-HEAD fingerprint for whichever repo it is given: the SAME
664:     # status capture the mirror already uses, PLUS the content manifest
665:     # of the paths that status names, combined by Get-CombinedSha256.
666:     # Used on the SOURCE for source_status_sha256 and on the MIRROR
667:     # itself for mirror_state_sha256 - one algorithm, so the two
668:     # fingerprints cannot drift apart by drifting the code that computes
669:     # them.
670:     #
671:     # THE CONTENT HALF IS NOT OPTIONAL, and measured 2026-08-04 rather
672:     # than assumed: editing an already-ignored file leaves the status
673:     # listing byte-identical, so a status-only fingerprint verified clean
674:     # across exactly the drift this check exists to catch. Status alone
675:     # sees a path APPEAR or DISAPPEAR or change code; it does not see the
676:     # bytes behind it change, and the bytes are the review input.
677:     $captured = Get-BaselineRaw $repo
678:     if (-not $captured.Ok) {
679:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = $captured.Reason }
680:     }
681:     $parsedStatus = ConvertTo-StatusRecord $captured.Fields
682:     if ($parsedStatus.ContainsKey("Error")) {
683:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = $parsedStatus.Error }
684:     }
685:     $subj = Get-ManifestSubject $parsedStatus.Records
686:     if ($subj.ContainsKey("Error")) {
687:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = $subj.Error }
688:     }
689:     $content = Get-ContentManifest $repo $subj.Paths
690:     if ($content.ContainsKey("Error")) {
691:         return @{ Ok = $false; Reason = $content.Error }
692:     }
693:     return @{ Ok = $true
694:               Sha = (Get-CombinedSha256 $captured.Fields $content.Paths) }
695: }
